We have found that the Rayleigh and Brillouin peaks are distinctly visible when the system is away from the critical point but the peaks tend to merge near the critical point. The sensitivity of the structure of the spectral function on wave vector
(k)
of the sound wave has been demonstrated. It has been shown that the Brillouin peaks get merged with the Rayleigh peak because of the absorption of sound waves in the vicinity of the critical point. As consequences, we have argued that Mach cone formation will be prevented and the flow harmonics are suppressed when the system passes near the CEP. We have also the shown mode dependent propagation of the perturbations which helps to find the speed of sound by looking at the position of the Brillouin peaks.

Critical slowing down is an important phenomena, cause the system to behave differently, should be included in the study of critical dynamics. The effects of critical slowing down is put into the calculations by considering a scalar non-hydrodynamic mode in the system. Therefore, we have derived the relevant equation of motions for the slow modes when
the extensivity condition of thermodynamics is unaltered. The role of extra out-of-equilibrium
\phi
mode on the dynamic structure factor near the QCD critical point has been investigated. The
\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\,\omega)
in presence of the
\phi
modes shows four peaks of Lorentzian type, which are asymmetrically situated in frequency axis with uneven magnitudes. While the
\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\,\omega)
without
\phi
modes admits three Lorentzian peaks, symmetrically situated on frequency axis with even magnitudes. We find that the asymmetry in the peaks originates due to coupling of the
\phi
modes with the hydrodynamic modes. The presence of the out-of-equilibrium modes can generate the extra peak in
\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\,\omega)
within the scope causal theory of hydrodynamics when the transverse modes (along with the longitudinal modes) are taken into account. This study may help in the experimental investigation of role of the out-of-equilibrium modes near any of the
\mathcal{O}(4)
critical points. The out-of-equilibrium mode plays a crucial role in reducing the width of the Lorentzians representing the thermal fluctuation which indicate the enhancement of the relaxation time of the system.

Along with the linear perturbations, we have also studied the nonlinear perturbations in the fluid. We have derived the equations for the propagation of nonlinear perturbations by the Reductive Perturbative method in MIS theory with considering all the relevant transport coefficients. We have derived relevant equations governing the propagation of nonlinear wave within the purview of second order causal hydrodynamics by taking into account the non-zero values of
\eta
,
\zeta
and
\kappa
in contrast to earlier works with nonlinear wave equations, where the effects of
\zeta
and
\kappa
were ignored
[
131
,
231
]
. In the presence of the CEP,
\zeta
and
\kappa
play significant roles as they tend to diverge near the CEP and hence can not be ignored. We observe, similar to the linear perturbation, the nonlinear perturbations too get suppressed near the CEP. The diverging nature of
\kappa
near the CEP plays dominant role in the suppression of the nonlinear wave. One expects the vanishing of Mach cone effects (or away side double-peak structure) and broadening of the two and three particle correlation as a consequence of the presence of the CEP. The suppression or collapse of elliptic flow will also indicated the existence of CEP. This may lead to the large event-by-event fluctuation of flow harmonics. Therefore, vanishing Mach cone effect (or away side double-peak structure) on away side jet and the enhancement of fluctuation of flow harmonics in event-by-event analysis accompanied by suppressed flow harmonics could be considered as signals of the CEP.

The possibility of existence and detection of the CEP has been studied in
[
162
]
. The mode coupling theory has been used to calculate the thermal conductivity at the location near and away from the QCD critical point in the
T-\mu
plane and shown that the
\kappa
diverges at the critical point. Authors in Ref.
[
162
]
have also demonstrated that the sharp change in
\kappa
at the critical point is eventually reflected in the two particle-correlation of fluctuations in rapidity space. Therefore, the study pave the way to confirm the existence of the CEP. The suppression of fluctuations in baryonic chemical potential (
\Delta\mu
) and temperature (
\Delta T
) due to the enhanced thermodynamic response functions at the CEP can signal the presence of the CEP
[
65
,
66
]
. The suppression in flow harmonics and suppression in fluctuations in
\Delta\mu
and
\Delta T
will be reflected through the spectra of hadrons and proton to pion ratio respectively.

In a real scenario, the possibility of the trajectories passing through the CEP may be infrequent, which restricts the magnitude of the fluctuations near the critical point. These fluctuations will stay out of equilibrium because of the expansion of the system and critical slowing down
[
70
,
71
]
. This problem has been analysed
[
264
]
in the evolution of hydrodynamic fluctuations in the system formed in RHIC-E. The emergence of the Kibble-Zurek length scale
[
265
,
266
]
and its relation with short range spatial correlations has been also discussed
[
264
]
. To note that the non-flow correlations get enhanced in presence of the CEP and such correlations may be measured as a function of
n/s
(ratio of particle density and entropy density) for detecting the CEP
[
264
]
.

The fluid dynamical description works in the region where the condition,
k<<q
is maintained (where
q
is the inverse of correlation length, expressed as
q=q_{o}\,r^{\nu}
,
q_{o}
is a constant and
\nu
is the critical index with a numerical value
\nu=0.73\pm 0.02

[
146
]
). The hydrodynamics becomes invalid when
\xi
diverges at the CEP. However, there could be a region of validity in the neighbourhood of the CEP where the predictions of fluid dynamical approach may be effective. Following procedure similar to the condensed matter physics
[
60
]
we can write,
k<<q_{o}r^{\nu}
for the region of validity of the hydrodynamics, which implies,

T>T_{c}\left[1+\left(\frac{k}{q_{o}}\right)^{1/\nu}\right]

(9.1)

As hydrodynamics is an effective theory for soft physics (small wave vector or large wavelength), the fluid dynamical description can be applied in the neighbourhood of the CEP but becomes inappropriate at the CEP due to the divergence of the correlation length. The response of the trajectories in the neighbourhood of the CEP in the
T-\mu
plane to the initial conditions away from equilibrium has been investigated in Ref.
[
250
]
.

In a realistic scenario, the matter formed in RHIC-E
evolves in space and time from the initial QGP phase to the final hadronic
freeze-out state through an intermediary phase transition.
The space time evolution of the locally equilibrated system is described
by relativistic viscous hydrodynamics. The experimentally detected signals
is the superposition of the yields for all the possible values
of temperatures and densities realised during the evolution of the system
from its initial to the freeze-out state.
The detection of CEP will require the disentanglement of contributions from the
neighbourhood (
\mu_{c},T_{c}
) from all other possible values of (
\mu,T
) which the system
confronts during its evolution history.
In the present work the expansion dynamics has not been taken into consideration, therefore,
the results obtained here can not be contrasted with experiments directly. The effects of the CEP with
(3+1) dimensional expansion within the scope of second order viscous hydrodynamics will be
next step in this direction.

The electromagnetic (EM) probes of QGP (see

[
27
]
for review)

i.e.
real photons and lepton pairs can be used to study the evolution of the
system from the pristine partonic stage to the
final hadronic stage through an intermediary
phase transition or crossover.
The photons and lepton can bring the information of the thermodynamic
state of their production points
[
27
,
28
]
efficiently as
their mean free paths are larger than the size of the fireball created
in RHIC-E. This fact should be contrasted with hadrons which are subjected to rescattering
in the medium and consequently loose information of their production point.
Therefore, in principle, the photons can bring the information
of the CEP very efficiently. In Ref.
[
267
]
the photon spectra
has been evaluated
by using second-order dissipative hydrodynamics
and the most updated rate of photon productions from QGP and
hadronic phases. However, the effects of the CEP on EoS, various transport
coefficients and response functions are required to be included
to get the imprints of CEP on the photon spectra which is beyond the
scope of the present work, although worth exploring and may be taken up in future endeavour.

Appendix A
Relativistic Hydrodynamics

A.1
Relaxation and coupling coefficients

In this appendix we provide the expressions for the relaxation and coupling coefficients
[
110
]

required to solve the Israel-Stewart hydrodynamical equations.

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(D_{41}D_{20}-D_{31}D_{30})\Lambda\phi\Omega J_{21}J_{31}~,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(J_{41}J_{42}-J_{31}J_{52})\Lambda\phi J_{21}J_{31}~,

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3\beta}{\phi^{2}\Omega^{2}}[5J_{52}-\frac{3}{D_{20}}\{J_{31}(J_{31}J_{30}-J_{41}J_{20})+J_{41}(J_{41}J_{10}-J_{31}J_{20})\}]~,

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{D_{41}}{\Lambda^{2}nmJ_{21}J_{31}}~,

\displaystyle\beta_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\beta J_{52}}{2\phi^{2}}~,

(A.1)

where,

\displaystyle D_{rs}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{r+1,s}J_{r-1,s}-(J_{rs})^{2},\,\,\,\,\phi=T(\epsilon+P),\,\,\,\,\psi=\frac{\epsilon+p}{n_{i}m_{i}},

\displaystyle\Lambda

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+5(\frac{\phi}{nm})-\psi^{2},\,\,\,\,\Omega=3\Big(\frac{\partial ln\phi}{\partial lnn}\Big)-5~,

(A.2)

where
r
,
s
are integers,
m_{i}
and
n_{i}
are respectively the mass and density of
i
-th type particle.
The quantities,
\phi,\psi
and
\Lambda
are calculated by using their relations with
\epsilon
,
p
and
n_{i}
.
We take the current quark mass of up and down flavors as 10 MeV.

J_{rs}
is defined as,

\displaystyle J_{rs}=\frac{A_{0}}{(2s+1)!!}\int_{0}^{\infty}N\Delta Sinh^{2(s+1)}\mathcal{R}Cosh^{r-2s}\mathcal{R}d\mathcal{R}~,

(A.3)

and

\displaystyle N=\frac{1}{exp(\beta Cosh\mathcal{R}-\alpha)-\epsilon}~,

(A.4)

where,

\displaystyle\Delta=1+\epsilon N,\,\,\,\,A_{0}=4\pi m_{i}^{3}~.

(A.5)

Appendix B
Propagation of perturbation near the QCD critical point

B.1
Thermodynamic derivatives

The expressions for
\omega_{\real}(k)
and
\omega_{\imaginary}(k)
contain derivatives of several thermodynamics variables. In this appendix
we recast these derivatives
in terms of response functions like: isothermal and adiabatic compressibilities (
\kappa_{T}
and
\kappa_{s}
), volume expansivity
\alpha_{p}

specific heats (
c_{p}
and
c_{v}
), baryon number susceptibility (
\chi_{B}
), velocity of sound (
c_{s}
), etc. The
baryon number density (
n
) and the entropy density (
s
) are given by

\displaystyle n=\Big(\frac{\partial p}{\partial\mu_{B}}\Big)_{T};\,\,\,\,\,s=\Big(\frac{\partial p}{\partial T}\Big)_{\mu_{B}}

(B.1)

Baryon number susceptibility (
\chi_{B}
), isothermal compressibility (
\kappa_{T}
), adiabatic compressibility (
\kappa_{s}
) and volume expansivity (
\alpha_{p}
) are given by,

\displaystyle\chi_{B}=\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu_{B}}\Big)_{T};\kappa_{T}=\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial p}\Big)_{T};\kappa_{s}=\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial p}\Big)_{s};\alpha_{p}=\frac{1}{V}\Big(\frac{\partial V}{\partial T}\Big)_{p}=-\frac{1}{n}\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)_{p}

(B.2)

Specific heats are given by

\displaystyle C_{P}=T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{p};C_{V}=T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{V}=T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{V}=\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

(B.3)

We have to express six quantities such as:
(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T})_{n},(\frac{\partial P}{\partial n})_{T},(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T})_{n},\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}
and
(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})_{\mu},(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}
in terms of various thermodynamic variables.

i) To evaluate:
(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T})_{n}
we start with

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(P,n)}{\partial(T,n)}=\frac{\partial(P,n)}{\partial(T,P)}\frac{\partial(T,P)}{\partial(s,P)}\frac{\partial(s,P)}{\partial(s,\epsilon)}\frac{\partial(s,\epsilon)}{\partial(s,n)}\frac{\partial(s,n)}{\partial(T,n)}

(B.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[-\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)_{P}\Big]\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial s}\Big)_{P}\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n\alpha_{P}\frac{T}{C_{P}}c^{2}_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}\frac{C_{V}}{T}=nc^{2}_{s}\alpha_{P}\frac{C_{V}}{C_{P}}\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}

Now if we use the relation

\displaystyle d\epsilon=Tds+\mu_{B}dn\,\,\,\,\,\text{and }\mu_{B}=\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}

(B.5)

then we can write

\displaystyle{\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=\mu_{B}nc^{2}_{s}\alpha_{P}\frac{C_{V}}{C_{p}}}

(B.6)

ii) Now consider
\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}
:

\displaystyle{\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}=\frac{1}{n\kappa_{T}}}

(B.7)

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(P,T)}{\partial(n,T)}=\frac{\partial(P,T)}{\partial(P,s)}\frac{\partial(P,s)}{\partial(\epsilon,s)}\frac{\partial(\epsilon,s)}{\partial(n,s)}\frac{\partial(n,s)}{\partial(n,T)}

(B.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial s}\Big)_{P}\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{T}{C_{P}}c^{2}_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}\frac{C_{V}}{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{B}c^{2}_{s}\frac{C_{V}}{C_{P}}

(B.9)

iii) The factor,
\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}
can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=c_{n}

(B.10)

For fixed net baryon number,
c_{n}
can be written as
c_{n}=c_{V}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=c_{V}}

(B.11)

iv)
\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}
can be estimated as:

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\partial(\epsilon,T)}{\partial(n,T)}=\frac{\partial(\epsilon,T)}{\partial(\epsilon,s)}\frac{\partial(\epsilon,s)}{\partial(n,s)}\frac{\partial(n,s)}{\partial(n,T)}

(B.12)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial s}\Big)_{\epsilon}\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial s}\Big)_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial s}\Big)_{\epsilon}\Big[\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial p}\Big)_{s}\Big(\frac{\partial p}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}\Big]\frac{c_{V}}{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial s}\Big)_{\epsilon}\Big[\frac{1}{c^{2}_{s}}\frac{1}{n\kappa_{s}}\Big]\frac{C_{V}}{T}=\frac{T}{c_{\epsilon}}\Big[\frac{1}{c^{2}_{s}}\frac{1}{n\kappa_{s}}\Big]\frac{C_{V}}{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{C_{V}}{c_{\epsilon}}\Big[\frac{1}{c^{2}_{s}}\frac{1}{n\kappa_{s}}\Big]

(B.13)

v) The quantity
\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu}\Big)_{T}
can be calculated as

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu}\Big)_{T}=\chi_{B}

(B.14)

vi) For
\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)_{\mu}
:

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)_{\mu}=-\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu}\Big)_{T}\Big(\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=-\chi_{B}\Big(\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

(B.15)

Now

\displaystyle sdT

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle dP-nd\mu\to dP=sdT+nd\mu

(B.16)

\displaystyle Tds

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle d\epsilon-\mu dn\to d\epsilon=Tds+\mu dn

(B.17)

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{sdT+nd\mu}{Tds+\mu dn}

(B.18)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{s+n\Big(\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial T}\Big)}{T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)+\mu\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)}

(B.19)

\displaystyle n\Big(\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial T}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)\Big[T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}+\mu\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)\Big]-s

(B.20)

Thus

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=\frac{c^{2}_{n}C_{V}-s}{n}\hskip 28.45274pt\text{where},c^{2}_{n}=\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)_{n}

(B.21)

Finally

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T}\Big)_{\mu}=\frac{\chi_{B}}{n}(s-c^{2}_{n}C_{V})

(B.22)

Appendix C
Dynamic structure factor near the QCD critical point

C.1
Expression for structure factor and the thermodynamic derivatives

In this appendix the expression for the dynamical spectral structure,
\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\,\omega)
derived by considering contributions
up to second order in transport coefficients (i.e
\eta^{2},\zeta^{2},\kappa^{2},\eta\zeta,\eta\kappa,\zeta\kappa
)
has been provided. The coupling and relaxation coefficients (
\tilde{\alpha_{0}},\tilde{\alpha_{1}},\beta_{0},\tilde{\beta_{1}},\beta_{2}
)
have been taken non-zero in obtaining the results displayed in the text, but have been taken
as zero in the following to avoid a more lengthy and complex expressions. Here we have used

\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)=\frac{\mathcal{S^{\prime}}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)}{\Big<\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\Big>}

(C.1)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S^{\prime}}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)=k^{2}n_{0}\Bigg[\omega^{2}n_{0}T^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big\{h_{0}\kappa T\omega+\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}k^{2}\Big(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta\Big)-k^{2}T^{2}\kappa\omega^{2}

\displaystyle+h_{0}kT\kappa\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}+k^{2}T\kappa(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)-T^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}^{2}\Big\}+k^{2}T\kappa h_{0}\Big\{-h_{0}T\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}

\displaystyle+k^{2}T\kappa(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)+T^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}^{2}\Big\}+n_{0}^{2}T^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}\times

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}k(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)+T^{3}\kappa\omega^{2}-h_{0}T\kappa\Big\}+k^{4}n_{0}h_{0}(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)^{2}h_{0}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}\times

\displaystyle\Big\{n_{0}T^{2}\omega^{2}\Big(k^{2}(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)+\kappa T\omega^{2}-k^{2}T\kappa\Big)\Big\}

\displaystyle+h_{0}^{2}\kappa T^{2}k^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}-n_{0}h_{0}\kappa T^{3}k^{2}\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}\Bigg]\Bigg/

\displaystyle\Bigg[h_{0}^{2}\Big\{k^{4}T^{2}\kappa^{2}\omega^{4}+n_{0}^{2}T^{2}\omega^{4}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big\}+h_{0}k^{2}\omega^{2}\kappa^{2}T^{4}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big\{\omega^{4}-2n_{0}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}\Big\}

\displaystyle-2k^{2}n_{0}^{2}T^{4}\kappa^{2}\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}^{2}-2n_{0}h_{0}k^{2}T^{4}\kappa^{2}\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}-2n_{0}^{3}T^{2}k^{4}\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\times

\displaystyle\Big\{\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}+\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}\Big\}+k^{4}n_{0}^{4}T^{2}\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}^{2}

\displaystyle+2k^{2}n_{0}T^{2}\kappa\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big\{k^{4}T\kappa\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}+T\omega^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}k^{2}(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)-T^{4}\kappa\omega^{2}\Big\}

\displaystyle+n_{0}^{2}T^{2}\Big\{h_{0}k^{2}\kappa^{2}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}^{2}+k^{2}T^{4}\kappa^{2}\omega^{4}-2k^{4}T^{2}\kappa\omega^{4}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}^{2}+2k^{2}h_{0}T\kappa\omega^{2}(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)

\displaystyle+\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}^{2}k^{4}(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)^{2}+2k^{4}T^{2}\kappa\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$P}}{\text{$\partial$T}}\Big)_{n}\Big(\frac{\text{$\partial$s}}{\text{$\partial$n}}\Big)_{T}(\zeta+\frac{4}{3}\eta)\Big\}\Bigg]\Big<\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\Big>

,

(C.2)

The expression for
\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)
contain derivatives of several thermodynamics quantities. In this appendix
we recast these derivatives
in terms of response functions like: isothermal and adiabatic compressibilities (
\kappa_{T}
and
\kappa_{s}
),
specific heats (
C_{p}
and
C_{v}
), baryon number susceptibility (
\chi_{B}
) and velocity of sound (
c_{s}
), etc. The
baryon number density (
n
) and the entropy density (
s
) can be written as:

\displaystyle n=\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\mu}\Big)_{T};\,\,\,\,\,s=\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T}\Big)_{\mu}

(C.3)

Baryon number susceptibility, isothermal compressibility and adiabatic compressibility are given by,

\displaystyle\chi_{B}=\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu}\Big)_{T};\kappa_{T}=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial P}\Big)_{T};\kappa_{s}=\frac{1}{n_{0}}\Big(\frac{\partial n}{\partial P}\Big)_{s}

(C.4)

Specific heats can be expressed as:

\displaystyle C_{P}=T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{P};C_{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{V}=T\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

(C.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{V}=\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}

The expression for partial derivatives,
(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T})_{n},(\frac{\partial P}{\partial n})_{T},(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T})_{n}
and
(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n})_{T}
are given in Eq.(
B.6
), Eq.(
B.7
), Eq.(
B.9
), Eq.(
B.11
) and Eq.(
B.13
)

Now we have to evaluate other quantities, such as
\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}
and
(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{n}
appear in
\mathcal{S}_{nn}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\,\omega)
in Eq.(
C.2
).

i)
\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}
is evaluated as:

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=\frac{1}{T}\Big(\frac{T_{0}\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=\frac{C_{V}}{T}

(C.6)

For fixed net baryon number,
c_{n}
can be written as
c_{n}=C_{V}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle{\Big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}=C_{V}}

(C.7)

ii) We evaluate the derivative
\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}
as

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial n}\Big)_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}=-\frac{1}{T}\Big(\frac{T\partial s}{\partial T}\Big)_{n}\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}

(C.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{C_{V}}{T}\frac{1}{n_{0}}\Big[n_{0}\Big(\frac{\partial T}{\partial n}\Big)_{s}\Big]=\frac{C_{V}}{n_{0}T\alpha_{s}}

The speed of sound is can be evaluated as:

\displaystyle c_{s}^{2}=\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)_{s/n}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{nd\mu+sdT}{\mu dn+Tds}

(C.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{nFdT+sdT}{\mu(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})_{\mu}dT+\mu(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}d\mu+T(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{\mu}dT+T(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T}d\mu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{nFdT+sdT}{\mu(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})_{\mu}dT+\mu F(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}dT+T(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{\mu}dT+TF(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T}dT}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{nF+s}{\mu(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})_{\mu}+\mu F(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}+T(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{\mu}+TF(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T}}

where,

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{\mu}-\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})_{\mu}\Big]\Big/\Big[{\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}-(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T}}\Big]

(C.10)

Appendix D
Understanding the parametric slow mode through the dynamic structure factor

D.1
Extensivity condition

The infinitesimal change in the total entropy for reversible process of a system of volume
V
and total conserved number
N
and total slow mode
\Phi
at temperature
T
and the chemical potential
\mu
is

dS

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(E+\text{PdV}-\mu N)/T-\pi d\Phi\,.

(D.1)

This will give

\displaystyle\frac{\partial S}{\partial E}\Bigg|_{N,V,\Phi}=\frac{1}{T},\,\,\frac{\partial S}{\partial V}\Bigg|_{N,E,\Phi}=\frac{P}{T},\,\,\frac{\partial S}{\partial N}\Bigg|_{E,V,\Phi}=-\frac{\mu}{T},\,\,\frac{\partial S}{\partial\Phi}\Bigg|_{E,V,N}=-\frac{\pi}{T}\,.

(D.2)

The total entropy
S
satisfies the following relation:

\displaystyle S(\lambda E,\lambda V,\lambda N,\lambda\Phi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lambda S(E,V,N,\Phi)\,.

(D.3)

Differentiation with respect to
\lambda
yield:

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\frac{\partial S}{\partial\lambda E}\Bigg|_{\lambda N,\lambda V,\lambda\Phi}+N\frac{\partial S}{\partial\lambda N}\Bigg|_{\lambda E,\lambda V,\lambda\Phi}+V\frac{\partial S}{\partial\lambda V}\Bigg|_{\lambda N,\lambda E,\lambda\Phi}+\Phi\frac{\partial S}{\partial\lambda\Phi}\Bigg|_{\lambda N,\lambda V,\lambda E}\,.

(D.4)

Putting
\lambda=1
and dividing by
V
we get,

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta(\epsilon+P-\mu n)-\pi\phi\,,

(D.5)

s=S/V
,
\epsilon=E/V
,
n=N/V
,
\phi=\Phi/V
are
the entropy density, energy density, number (baryon) denisty,
and the density of the scalar field,
\phi
.
Eq. (
D.5
) and Eq. (
7.4
) give,

\displaystyle dP

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle sdT+nd\mu+\phi d(T\pi)\,.

(D.6)

Appendix E
Understanding the parametric slow mode through the dynamic structure factor

E.1
The set linear equations derived for the perturbations in
\omega-k
space

The linearized system of equations obtained
by using the Fourier-Laplace transformation
of the perturbative quantities (generically denoted
by
\delta Q
) as:

\delta{Q}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)=\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\,d^{3}r\int_{0}^{\infty}\,dte^{-i(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}\cdot r+\omega t)}\delta\tilde{Q}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$r$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$r$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle r$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle r$}},t).

(E.1)

and

\delta{Q}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\,0)=\delta{Q}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},t=0)=\int^{\infty}_{-\infty}\,d^{3}re^{-i(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}}\cdot r)}\delta\tilde{Q}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$r$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$r$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle r$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle r$}},t=0).

(E.2)

The system of equations for the perturbations in hydrodynamic and
non-hydrodynamic (
\phi
) field in
\omega-k
space are given
below.

\displaystyle i\omega\delta\epsilon(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\delta q_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)=-\delta{\epsilon}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.3)

\displaystyle i\omega(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik\delta P(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle ik\delta\Pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+i\omega\delta q_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik\delta\pi_{||\,||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

(E.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){\delta u}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-\delta{q}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

\displaystyle-i\omega

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})\delta u_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-i\omega\delta q_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik\delta\pi_{\perp\,||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

(E.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})\delta{u}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-{\delta q}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

\displaystyle i\omega\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ikn_{0}\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)=-{\delta n}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.6)

\displaystyle\big(1+i\omega\frac{1}{3}\zeta\beta_{0}\big)\delta\Pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\frac{1}{3}\zeta\big(\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-{\alpha}_{0}\delta q_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)\big)=-\frac{1}{3}\zeta\beta_{0}{\delta\Pi}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.7)

\displaystyle(1+i\omega\kappa T_{0}\beta_{1})\delta q_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\kappa T_{0}\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\big[\kappa-C_{T\pi}K_{q\pi}T_{0}^{2}\big]\delta T(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle-ikT_{0}^{2}K_{q\pi}C_{\phi\pi}\delta\phi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik\kappa T_{0}{\alpha}_{0}\delta\Pi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ikT_{0}^{2}K_{q\pi}C_{n\pi}\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle-ik\kappa T_{0}{\alpha}_{1}\delta\pi_{||\,||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)=-\kappa T_{0}{\delta u}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-\beta_{1}\kappa T_{0}{\delta q}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.8)

\displaystyle(1+i\omega\beta_{1}kT_{0})\delta q_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+i\kappa T_{0}(\omega\delta u_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-k{\alpha}_{1}\delta\pi_{\perp||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)\big)

(E.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\kappa T_{0}\big[\delta{u}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-\beta_{1}{\delta q}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\big]

\displaystyle(1+2i\omega\eta\beta_{2})\delta\pi_{||\,||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\frac{4}{3}\eta\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-\frac{4}{3}\delta q_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle=-2\eta\beta_{2}{\delta\pi}_{||\,||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.10)

\displaystyle(1+2i\omega\eta\beta_{2})\delta\pi_{\perp\,\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik\frac{4}{3}\eta\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\frac{4}{3}\eta{\alpha}_{1}\delta q_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle=-2\eta\beta_{2}{\delta\pi}_{\perp\,\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.11)

\displaystyle(1+2i\omega\eta\beta_{2})\delta\pi_{\perp\,\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+ik\eta\delta u_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik\delta q_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle=-2\eta\beta_{2}{\delta\pi}_{\perp\,||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0),

(E.12)

\displaystyle(i\omega+C_{\phi\pi}(\gamma-T_{0}^{2}\frac{K_{q\pi}}{\kappa}k^{2}))\delta\phi(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)+\{\gamma-K_{q\pi}(T_{0}^{2}\frac{K_{q\pi}}{\kappa}-\frac{1}{C_{T\pi}})k^{2}\}C_{T\pi}\delta T(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle+(\gamma-T_{0}^{2}\frac{K_{q\pi}^{2}}{\kappa}k^{2})C_{n\pi}\delta n(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)-ik({\phi}-i\omega T_{0}K_{q\pi})\delta u_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},\omega)

\displaystyle=-(1+ik\frac{K_{q\pi}^{2}}{\kappa}T_{0}C_{\phi\pi}){\delta\phi}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-ikT_{0}K_{q\pi}{\delta u}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\,,

(E.13)

where, subscripts ”
||
” and ”
\perp
” stand for projection along and perpendicular to
k
respectively. After expressing
\delta\epsilon
and
\delta p
as:

\displaystyle\delta\epsilon

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial n}\big)\delta n+\big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial T}\big)\delta T+\big(\frac{\partial\epsilon}{\partial\phi}\big)\delta\phi,

(E.14)

\displaystyle\delta P

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial n}\big)\delta n+\big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial T}\big)\delta T+\big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\phi}\big)\delta\phi\,.

(E.15)

The equations can be arranged to have the matrix from with matrix:

\mathcal{M}\,\mathcal{\delta Q}=\mathcal{A}

(E.16)

where,
\mathcal{M}
, is an
11\times 11
matrix representing the
coefficients of the column vector comprinsing the
quantities,
\delta n,\delta T,\delta u_{||},\delta u_{\perp},\delta\Pi,\delta q_{||}
,
\delta q_{\perp},\delta\pi_{||\,||},\delta\pi_{||\,\perp},\delta\pi_{\perp\,\perp}
, and
\delta\phi
.

{\delta\mathcal{Q}}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\delta n\\
\delta T\\
\delta\phi\\
\delta u_{||}\\
\delta q_{||}\\
\delta\pi\\
\delta\pi_{||||}\\
\delta\pi_{\perp\perp}\\
\delta u_{\perp}\\
\delta q_{\perp}\\
\delta\pi_{||\perp}\end{array}\right),\,\,\,\,\mathcal{A}=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}-{\delta{n}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-\epsilon_{n}{\delta{n}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-\epsilon_{\phi}\delta{\phi}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-e_{T}{\delta{T}}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-\delta{\phi}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-ikT_{0}{\delta{u}}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\kappa_{{q\pi}}\\
(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){\delta{u}}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-{\delta{q}}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-\chi\beta_{1}T_{0}{\delta{q}}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-T_{0}\chi{\delta{u}}_{||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-\frac{1}{3}\beta_{0}\zeta\delta{\pi}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-2\beta_{2}\eta\delta{\pi}_{||||}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-2\beta_{2}\eta\delta{\pi}_{\perp\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){\delta{u}}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-{\delta{q}}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-\chi\beta_{1}T_{0}{\delta{q}}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)-T_{0}\chi{\delta{u}}_{\perp}(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\\
-2\beta_{2}\eta\delta{\pi}_{||}\perp(\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle k$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle k$}},0)\end{array}\right)

(E.17)

Appendix F
Dynamic structure factor near the QCD critical point

F.1
Speed of sound and the roots of
\omega(k)

In this appendix we provide the expressions for the speed of sound
and the roots of
\omega(k)
with the inclusion of
out-of-equilibrium mode
\phi
.
The speed of sound (
c_{s}
) is given by,

\displaystyle c_{s}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\partial P}{\partial\epsilon}\Big)_{s/n}

(F.1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{sdT+nd\mu+\phi d\pi}{Tds+\mu dn+\pi d\phi}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}/\mathcal{B}\,,

where,
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
are given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}={s+n\mathcal{F}+\phi\mathcal{G}}\,,

(F.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{\mu,\pi}+T\mathcal{F}(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T,\pi}+T\mathcal{G}(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\pi})_{T,\mu}+\mu(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})_{\mu,\pi}+\mu\mathcal{F}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T,\pi}

(F.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu\mathcal{G}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\pi})_{T,\mu}+T(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})_{\mu,\pi}+T\mathcal{F}(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T,\pi}+T\mathcal{G}(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\pi})_{T,\mu}\,.

where,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Big[(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})-\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})\Big]+(\frac{\partial\pi}{\partial T})\Big[(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\pi})-\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\pi})\Big]}{\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}-(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T}}\,,

(F.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Big[(\frac{\partial s}{\partial T})-\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial T})\Big]+(\frac{\partial\mu}{\partial T})\Big[(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})-\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})\Big]}{\frac{s}{n}(\frac{\partial n}{\partial\mu})_{T}-(\frac{\partial s}{\partial\mu})_{T}}\,.

(F.5)

The four roots of
\omega
indicating the location of peaks
in the structure factor with scalar field (
\phi
), obtained from the dispersion relation are
given below.

\displaystyle\omega_{1}=\frac{-\sqrt{-(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})^{2}}+i\epsilon_{0}+iP_{0}}{2T_{0}\chi}+\frac{\eta k^{2}\sqrt{-(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){}^{2}}}{(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(k^{3})\,,

(F.6)

\displaystyle\omega_{2}=\frac{\sqrt{-(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){}^{2}}+i\epsilon_{0}+iP_{0}}{2T_{0}\chi}-\frac{\eta k^{2}\sqrt{-(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){}^{2}}}{(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0}){}^{2}}+\mathcal{O}(k^{3})\,,

(F.7)

\displaystyle\omega_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i\gamma(C_{\phi\pi}\epsilon_{T}-C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{\phi})}{3\epsilon_{T}}+\frac{ik^{2}}{9\epsilon_{T}(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})}\Big[3T_{0}^{2}\bar{\phi}C_{\phi\pi}\epsilon_{T}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3T_{0}^{2}\bar{\phi}C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3\chi\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{\phi}

(F.8)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3T_{0}^{2}C_{{n\pi}}\epsilon_{T}n_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3T_{0}^{2}C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{n}n_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3\chi\epsilon_{n}n_{0}-3\epsilon_{\phi}P_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 3T_{0}\epsilon_{\phi}P_{T}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3T_{0}\epsilon_{T}P_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+\zeta\epsilon_{T}+4\eta\epsilon_{T}+3T_{0}\chi P_{T}\Big]+\mathcal{O}(k^{3})\,,

\displaystyle\omega_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{i\gamma(C_{\phi\pi}\epsilon_{T}-C_{{T\pi}}e_{\phi})}{3\epsilon_{T}}+k\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{\phi}P_{T}-\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{T}P_{\phi}+\epsilon_{T}n_{0}P_{n}-\epsilon_{n}n_{0}P_{T}+\epsilon_{0}P_{T}+P_{0}P_{T}}{\epsilon_{T}(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})}}

(F.9)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{ik^{2}}{{9\epsilon_{T}(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})}}\Big[(3T_{0}^{2}\bar{\phi}C_{\phi\pi}\epsilon_{T}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3T_{0}^{2}\bar{\phi}C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3\chi\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{\phi}-3T_{0}^{2}C_{{n\pi}}\epsilon_{T}n_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3T_{0}^{2}C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{n}n_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3\chi\epsilon_{n}n_{0}-3\epsilon_{\phi}P_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3T_{0}\epsilon_{\phi}P_{T}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3T_{0}\epsilon_{T}P_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\zeta\epsilon_{T}+4\eta\epsilon_{T}+3T_{0}\chi P_{T}\Big]+\mathcal{O}(k^{3})\,.

The width of the Brillouin peaks can be identified as

\displaystyle\Gamma_{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-{9\epsilon_{T}(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})}\Big[3T_{0}^{2}\bar{\phi}C_{\phi\pi}\epsilon_{T}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3T_{0}^{2}\bar{\phi}C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3\chi\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{\phi}-3T_{0}^{2}C_{{n\pi}}\epsilon_{T}n_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}

(F.10)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 3T_{0}^{2}C_{{T\pi}}\epsilon_{n}n_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3\chi\epsilon_{n}n_{0}-3\epsilon_{\phi}P_{0}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3\epsilon_{0}\epsilon_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}-3T_{0}\epsilon_{\phi}P_{T}\kappa_{{q\pi}}+3T_{0}\epsilon_{T}P_{\phi}\kappa_{{q\pi}}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\zeta\epsilon_{T}+4\eta\epsilon_{T}+3T_{0}\chi P_{T}\Big]\,.

The speed of sound obtained from the dispersion relation is expressed as:

\displaystyle c^{2}_{s}=\frac{\epsilon_{T}n_{0}P_{n}-\epsilon_{n}n_{0}P_{T}+\epsilon_{0}P_{T}+P_{0}P_{T}+\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{\phi}P_{T}-\bar{\phi}\epsilon_{T}P_{\phi}}{\epsilon_{T}(\epsilon_{0}+P_{0})}\,.

(F.11)

where, in Eqs.(
F.8
)-(
F.11
), we have used the notation as

\displaystyle X_{Y}=\Big(\frac{\partial X}{\partial Y}\Big)\,.

(F.12)
